{"forms":[{"form":"more heteroclitic","tags":["comparative"]},{"form":"most heteroclitic","tags":["superlative"]}],"lang_code":"en","pos":"adj","senses":[{"glosses":["Irregular in inflection."],"topics":["human-sciences","linguistics","sciences"]},{"examples":[{"text":"The Proto-Indo-European term *wódr̥ (“water”) is typical of nouns with heteroclitic stems: the strong stem *wódr̥- occurs in the direct cases while the weak stem *wédn̥- is found instead in the oblique cases, hence the contrast between nominative *wódr̥ and genitive *wédn̥s.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["Signifying a nominal stem which alternates between more than one form when declined for grammatical case, particularly in the suffix (such as between *-r̥- in the strong stem and *-n̥- in the weak stem)."],"tags":["Indo-European-studies"]},{"glosses":["Pertaining to antibodies that react to a wide variety of antigens."],"topics":["biology","microbiology","natural-sciences"]}],"upstream_line":449263,"upstream_offset":2119948722,"upstream_record_sha256":"72186e9d2ca177bd3b3059aa7fa6d733d4e6e723b7342b89ee3cf83391c2af02","word":"heteroclitic"}
